I honestly believe I'm destined to completely revolutionize how people use this technology.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt so convinced of this unique vision that you took actions that were clearly dangerous or entirely out of character, such as believing you had abilities that defied the laws of physics or common logic?\n\nPatient: I don't believe I have supernatural powers that defy physics, but I’ve been so sure of my vision that I pushed unsafe work timelines that scared my crew and bought expensive equipment on credit. The breaking point came when a major customer complained about chaotic scheduling and Alfred’s increasingly confrontational, \"wired\" demeanor on-site, prompting his employer to pull him from field work; his wife, worried about his drastic change and inability to slow down or sleep, brought him to a psychiatrist after his apprenticeship mentees reported being scared by his intensity.\n- Chief Complaint: “I can’t seem to slow down—I’m constantly taking on new projects and working nonstop, like I’m stuck in overdrive.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): Lately I swing from feeling on top of the world and like everyone should just get on my level, to snapping at my wife or my crew over the smallest thing, like someone misplacing a screwdriver, and once I start yelling I feel almost justified, like they’re the ones slowing down my big plans.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): It’s like my body and brain are stuck in high gear—after my shift I’ll hit the books for the Master Electrician test, then head to the garage to sketch out new business ideas and start wiring some new gadget, and before I know it the sun’s coming up and I’ve got three half-finished projects going and I’m still looking for more to do.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): I honestly feel like I’m meant to revolutionize how people use solar and smart homes around here, like I can see solutions other electricians just aren’t smart enough to understand, so I ignore my boss and my wife when they tell me to slow down because I’m sure they’ll thank me once they see how big this is.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): Most nights I only lie down for two or three hours, if that, and even then my mind is racing with wiring diagrams and business plans so I just get back up, make coffee, and keep working because I don’t feel tired at all, just wired and almost annoyed at the idea of wasting time sleeping.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I’m trying to focus on wiring a panel or studying, every little thing yanks my attention away—the hum of a light, a text buzz, a new idea popping in my head—and I’ll drop what I’m doing halfway through to check the noise, start a new Google search, or sketch another design, until everything around me is a mess of started jobs I can’t seem to finish.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
